That's just normal, rational worry. That's just the reality of having to constantly be on guard in your own home because someone is actively trying to dismantle your life piece by piece while everyone else looks the other way. I want to sleep. I want to just close my eyes and rest like a normal guy. I definitely don't have any extra energy to spare, I can promise you that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever used methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other recreational drugs or new medications around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Methamphetamine? Cocaine? Doc, I... I don't even know what to say to that. Look, I don't know what kind of people usually sit in that chair across from you, but you have definitely got the wrong guy. I am a hardworking, tax-paying man. I've been at the same fabrication shop for over thirty years. \n\nDo you have any idea what would happen if someone showed up to a metalworking shop hopped up on meth or cocaine? You'd lose an arm. You'd get someone else killed. We are operating heavy hydraulic presses, we're using shears that can slice right through half-inch steel plate like it's warm butter. You have to be sharp. You have to be completely focused. The shop foreman, Gary, he runs random drug tests every single quarter, and I have never failed one in my entire life. I've never even smoked marijuana. Not even back in the eighties when half the guys on the floor were doing it. It's just not who I am. I might have a cold beer on the porch on a Friday evening, but that is the absolute extent of it. \n\nAnd as for new medications, no, absolutely not. I don't like taking pills. I take a standard multivitamin that my wife picks up at the grocery store, and if my lower back is really screaming at me after a long shift hunched over the welding table, I might take a couple of ibuprofen. That's it. My regular doctor hasn't prescribed me anything new, I haven't started taking any weird supplements or anything like that. \n\nSo if you're asking this because you think I'm hallucinating all this stuff because I'm on drugs, you are barking way up the wrong tree. My head is completely clear. That's exactly what I keep trying to explain to everybody. I am completely sober, completely rational, and completely aware of my surroundings. It's honestly a little insulting that my family drags me in here, treating me like I'm crazy, and now I'm having to defend myself against being some kind of drug addict, all just because I'm the only one who actually pays attention to what's happening in my own house. I don't need drugs to see that my tools have been messed with. I just need my own two eyes.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs been present for at least one month?\n\nPatient: One month? Doc, if this had only been going on for a month, I probably wouldn't even be sitting here right now because I'd still be trying to give the guy the benefit of the doubt. I'd still be telling myself, \"Oh, maybe I really did just misplace that wrench,\" or \"Maybe the shed door just blew open in the wind.\" \n\nNo, no, it has been way, way longer than a month. We are talking about nine months. Nine solid months of this... this absolute nightmare. \n\nIt started exactly—and I mean exactly—when my wife's relative moved into our home. Before he dragged his suitcases through my front door, my life was completely normal. I had peace. I'd come home from the fabrication shop, have my dinner, head out to the garage to tinker with my gear, and everything was right where I left it. My house was my castle, just like they say. But for the last nine months? Every single day is a test. Every single day I have to wake up wondering what he's going to sabotage next, how he's going to try and make me look like a fool in front of my own wife. \n\nYou try living on high alert in your own living room for nine months, Doc. It wears a man down. It’s not just some passing phase or a bad week. It’s not a \"belief\" that just popped into my head recently. It’s been nearly a year of him plotting and scheming right under my roof, day after day, week after week. Nine months of me having to double-check my own toolboxes and watch my back in my own kitchen. So yeah, it's been a lot longer than a month, I can promise you that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there is any implausible or impossible explanation for these events, or is it only that this one person is secretly messing with your things?\n\nPatient: Implausible? Like... what, like ghosts or aliens or the CIA or something? No, no, Doc, let's keep our feet on the ground here. I'm a practical man. I deal with steel and rivets, not science fiction. There's nothing impossible about it, and that's exactly why it's so incredibly frustrating that I'm sitting here. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I'm just making up fairy tales in my head. But I know—I absolutely know—that my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. \n\nIt's just him. Just a regular guy with a mean streak and too much time on his hands. By accident? Come on. You don't have accidents like that every single weekend for nine straight months. \n\nHe's doing it on purpose, and there's no crazy sci-fi explanation for it. After all these months of watching him operate, I can see plain as day what his end game is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. He gets the run of the place. He gets the house, he gets my garage, he gets the comfortable life I’ve spent thirty years breaking my back to build. There's nothing impossible about it, Doc. I'll get my tackle box ready on a Friday night, check the lines, make sure I've got my lures all set up for bass down at the reservoir, and come Saturday morning at 4:30 AM, suddenly the latch on the box is jammed shut with superglue, or my good spinning reel is mysteriously missing the handle. He gets the run of the place. He gets the house, he gets my garage, he gets the comfortable life I’ve spent thirty years breaking my back to build. There's nothing impossible about it, Doc. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Clear evidence? What kind of evidence are we even talking about here? Cameras? Because let me tell you, Doc, if you put up a camera in the garage, he’s just going to find the blind spot. He’s sneaky like that. And you say evidence of 'no one entering the house'—well, he already lives there! He’s in the guest room right down the hall from the kitchen, so he doesn't need to break in. He doesn't need to pick a lock. He just waits until I'm dead asleep. 

Look, this... this right here is exactly why I’m sitting in this chair. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My wife, her family, and now you're talking about showing me hypothetical 'evidence' to try and prove that I'm just seeing things. But I know—I absolutely know without a single shadow of a doubt—that my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. 

You can show me whatever paperwork or video or whatever it is you think you have, but it doesn't change the basic laws of physics. My tools don’t move themselves. They just don't. I've worked in fabrication my whole life, I know exactly where I put my gear, down to the millimeter. And my fishing plans? They don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. A latch doesn't just accidentally glue itself shut on a Saturday morning. A reel doesn't unscrew its own handle and walk away. It takes a person to do that. 

And after all these months of dealing with this, of watching him operate, of seeing the little smirk on his face when my wife tells me I'm being paranoid... I can see plain as day what his plan is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. That's the bottom line. He wants me gone so he can have my life, my house, my garage. So no, Doc, no 'evidence' is going to change my mind, because I'm the one living in the middle of this warzone, and I know exactly who the enemy is.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, Doc... I’m really only here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining the whole thing. I know for an absolute fact that my wife’s relative has been coming into this house just to get at me. \n\nI mean, I work as a fabricator, right? I’ve been assembling parts and working with my hands my whole life. I am a very organized guy. I know exactly where I leave my equipment. But I'll go out to my garage, and my things are shifted around. My tools don’t just move themselves. A socket wrench doesn't just sprout legs and walk across the workbench, you know? But everyone just says, \"Oh, you just misplaced it, you're getting older.\" No, I didn't misplace a thing. \n\nAnd it’s not just the garage, either. Every single weekend—and I mean every weekend without fail—my fishing plans get completely spoiled by some \"accident.\" My gear is messed with, the line is tangled up, or something is suddenly wrong with the truck. That is not a coincidence. After all these months of this happening, day in and day out, I can see plain as day what's going on. He’s been trying to take over my place. He wants to push me out of my own home, just edge me right out of the picture entirely, and nobody wants to listen to me about it. They just sit there and tell me I'm crazy, but I'm the one living it.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that this is really happening, if at all?\n\nPatient: How certain am I? Doc, I am one hundred percent, absolutely, unequivocally certain. There is not a single shadow of a doubt in my mind. I mean, look at it this way. If you walked into your office here, right, and your pens were moved from the left side of your desk to the right, and your chair was lowered three inches, and your files were out of alphabetical order, would you just say, 'Oh, well, I guess I'm just losing my mind'? No, you'd know someone had been in here. \n\nThat's exactly what I'm dealing with. I have been an assembler for thirty-two years. Thirty-two years of putting things together, exactly where they belong, piece by piece. My brain works like a blueprint. I don't misplace a 5/8 inch wrench. I don't accidentally leave the chuck key out of the drill press. But I go out there, and it's moved. And my wife's relative—he was over here for Thanksgiving, right? And ever since then, it's been this slow, methodical campaign. \n\nIt’s not just the garage, like I said. It’s the fishing gear. Every Saturday morning, I wake up at 4:30 AM. I make my coffee—black, no sugar—and I go out to load the truck. And every single time, without fail, something is wrong. Last week, the spark plug wires on my outboard motor were swapped. Swapped! You don't just accidentally swap spark plug wires. Two weeks before that, someone had let exactly ten pounds of air out of my front left tire. Just enough to be a problem, not enough to be obvious right away. \n\nAnd my wife, she just sighs and rolls her eyes and tells me I'm being paranoid. But I see what he's doing. It's so clear to me. He wants to make me look incompetent. He wants her to think I'm losing it, so he can step in, be the man of the house, and push me right out the door. I’ve seen the way he looks around my property, sizing it up. I know it sounds crazy to you, and I know it sounds crazy to my family, but I am living it every single day. I am as certain of this as I am that the sun is going to come up tomorrow morning. There is no 'if at all' about it. It is happening.\n\nDoctor: Do any other people in your family or community believe this is happening too?\n\nPatient: Not a single one of them, Doc. And that... well, that right there is exactly why I’m sitting in this chair talking to you today. I’m only here because everybody—and I mean everybody, my wife, my kids, the neighbors—keeps acting like I’m imagining the whole thing. It’s like talking to a brick wall. I try to sit my wife down, right? We'll be at the kitchen table, and I’ll say, 'Look at this. Look at how the workbench was left.' And she just sighs, gets that pitying look on her face, and tells me to let it go. Like I'm a child making up ghost stories in the dark. \n\nBut I know what I know. I know my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. It's not a theory, it's a fact. But they all just gang up and say, 'Oh, you're working too hard, you're just stressed out from the shop.' Stressed? Does stress pick up a heavy-duty torque wrench and move it to the opposite side of the garage? Because my tools don’t move themselves! I’ve been working with my hands for thirty-two years, I know how gravity works. \n\nAnd I try to tell them about the weekends, too. I lay it all out for them, completely logically. I explain that my fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. Once? Sure, maybe you get a tangled line. Twice? Bad luck. But every weekend for months? You'd think after the fifth or sixth time the truck gets messed with, someone in my family would say, 'Hey, maybe he's onto something.' But no. They just look at me like I'm the one losing my grip. \n\nIt’s isolating, Doc, I’ll tell you that much. You work your whole life, providing for your family, keeping your house in perfect order, and then this happens. After all these months of this going on, I can see plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place. He wants to push me out. He plays the nice guy to them, smiling and making small talk, and meanwhile, he's methodically trying to erase me from my own life so he can step right into my shoes. And nobody—not one single person—will just open their eyes and see what's happening right in front of them.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard or seen things that other people around you do not?\n\nPatient: No, no, no. Doc, I think you're misunderstanding me. I'm not... I don't hear voices whispering in the walls, and I'm not seeing ghosts or shadows or anything like that. I know exactly what you're getting at with a question like that, and let me tell you, my mind is not playing tricks on me. My hearing is fine, my vision is fine. \n\nWhat I see is exactly what is there in the physical world. Real, tangible things. When I go out to the garage, I'm not imagining that my 5/8 inch wrench is sitting on the wrong shelf. It is physically sitting there. Anyone could see it if they just bothered to actually look! If my wife walked out there and actually looked at the pegboard instead of just rolling her eyes, she would see it too. But she doesn't. \n\nIn my line of work—fabrication, assembling—you have to trust your eyes and your ears. If a machine has a bad bearing, you hear the rattle. It's a real sound. If a piece of sheet metal is cut a sixteenth of an inch off, you see it. You don't imagine it, it's just a fact of the material. And that's how I operate. I deal in hard facts. So when I look under the hood of my truck and see the spark plug wires swapped, I am looking at real rubber and real wire. I can touch them. I can hold them in my hands. \n\nI don't hear things other people don't hear. I hear my wife sighing at me. I hear the neighbor's dog barking. I hear real things. And I see real things. I see that relative of hers sizing up my living room. I see the tire on my truck sitting a little lower because exactly ten pounds of air is gone. It’s all real, physical evidence, Doc. So no, I'm not making things up in my head. Everything I'm dealing with is happening right here in the real world, in broad daylight. It's just that I'm the only one willing to actually open my eyes and look at the evidence.\n\nDoctor: Besides this one person, do you believe anyone else is targeting you or trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's not... look, I'm not one of those guys who thinks the CIA is tapping my phone or the mailman is poisoning my water. I'm a rational man. I don't need to invent some giant, crazy conspiracy with a whole army of people out to get me when the truth is sitting right there in my own living room. It's just him. Just my wife's relative. He doesn't need anyone else helping him because he’s sneaky enough on his own, playing the long game. \n\nAnd I know I keep coming back to this, Doc, and I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record here, but you have to understand—I’m only sitting in this office because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My whole family is treating me like I'm the one with the problem. But I know what I know. I know my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. He doesn't need accomplices to sneak into my garage. Like I said, my tools don’t move themselves. A heavy-duty wrench doesn't just sprout legs and walk to a different workbench. My fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. It's a targeted, one-man operation. \n\nWhy would he involve anybody else? That would just mean sharing the prize, right? And after all these months of dealing with this, day in and day out, I can see plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. He wants my life. He wants to be the one sitting at the head of my dinner table, being the man of the house. He wants my wife to look at him like he's the capable one, the one who doesn't 'misplace' his tools or mess up the outboard motor. \n\nSo, no. It's not the neighbors. It's not the guys down at the fabrication shop, they're good guys, we've worked together for years. It is just him. He is the one doing it, and he's doing it right under everyone's noses, smiling at my wife while he takes a sledgehammer to my life, and it just drives me up the wall that nobody else will open their eyes and see it.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been having periods of feeling unusually energized or needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Energized? No, no, I don't know where you'd get an idea like that, Doc. If anything, I'm just flat-out exhausted from having to deal with all this nonsense day in and day out. \n\nI mean, look, I'm a working man. I've been down at the fabrication shop for over three decades. My alarm goes off at 5:15 AM every single morning, same as it always has. I get up, I brew my coffee, and I'm out the door. And let me tell you, when you spend eight, sometimes ten hours a day on your feet, working with heavy shears and presses and wrestling with sheet metal... by the time you get home, your bones are aching and you are just beat. \n\nSo no, I'm not bouncing off the walls or staying up all night doing jumping jacks. I need my sleep. You can't be sleep-deprived around industrial equipment, or you'll end up losing a finger, or worse. I usually try to be in bed, boots off, lights out, by 10:00 PM sharp. \n\nNow, do I always fall right to sleep the second my head hits the pillow? Well, no, of course not. Sometimes I'll lie there staring at the ceiling for a while, but that's just because my mind is turning over the facts. I'll be trying to figure out how he managed to bypass the padlock on the shed without leaving a scratch, or I'll be running through a mental checklist of what I need to double-check and secure the next morning so my weekend doesn't get ruined again. \n\nBut that's not... that's not having \"too much energy\" or needing less sleep. That's just normal, rational worry. That's just the reality of having to constantly be on guard in your own home because someone is actively trying to dismantle your life piece by piece while everyone else looks the other way. I want to sleep. I want to just close my eyes and rest like a normal guy. I definitely don't have any extra energy to spare, I can promise you that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever used methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other recreational drugs or new medications around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Methamphetamine? Cocaine? Doc, I... I don't even know what to say to that. Look, I don't know what kind of people usually sit in that chair across from you, but you have definitely got the wrong guy. I am a hardworking, tax-paying man. I've been at the same fabrication shop for over thirty years. \n\nDo you have any idea what would happen if someone showed up to a metalworking shop hopped up on meth or cocaine? You'd lose an arm. You'd get someone else killed. We are operating heavy hydraulic presses, we're using shears that can slice right through half-inch steel plate like it's warm butter. You have to be sharp. You have to be completely focused. The shop foreman, Gary, he runs random drug tests every single quarter, and I have never failed one in my entire life. I've never even smoked marijuana. Not even back in the eighties when half the guys on the floor were doing it. It's just not who I am. I might have a cold beer on the porch on a Friday evening, but that is the absolute extent of it. \n\nAnd as for new medications, no, absolutely not. I don't like taking pills. I take a standard multivitamin that my wife picks up at the grocery store, and if my lower back is really screaming at me after a long shift hunched over the welding table, I might take a couple of ibuprofen. That's it. My regular doctor hasn't prescribed me anything new, I haven't started taking any weird supplements or anything like that. \n\nSo if you're asking this because you think I'm hallucinating all this stuff because I'm on drugs, you are barking way up the wrong tree. My head is completely clear. That's exactly what I keep trying to explain to everybody. I am completely sober, completely rational, and completely aware of my surroundings. It's honestly a little insulting that my family drags me in here, treating me like I'm crazy, and now I'm having to defend myself against being some kind of drug addict, all just because I'm the only one who actually pays attention to what's happening in my own house. I don't need drugs to see that my tools have been messed with. I just need my own two eyes.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs been present for at least one month?\n\nPatient: One month? Doc, if this had only been going on for a month, I probably wouldn't even be sitting here right now because I'd still be trying to give the guy the benefit of the doubt. I'd still be telling myself, \"Oh, maybe I really did just misplace that wrench,\" or \"Maybe the shed door just blew open in the wind.\" \n\nNo, no, it has been way, way longer than a month. We are talking about nine months. Nine solid months of this... this absolute nightmare. \n\nIt started exactly—and I mean exactly—when my wife's relative moved into our home. Before he dragged his suitcases through my front door, my life was completely normal. I had peace. I'd come home from the fabrication shop, have my dinner, head out to the garage to tinker with my gear, and everything was right where I left it. My house was my castle, just like they say. But for the last nine months? Every single day is a test. Every single day I have to wake up wondering what he's going to sabotage next, how he's going to try and make me look like a fool in front of my own wife. \n\nYou try living on high alert in your own living room for nine months, Doc. It wears a man down. It’s not just some passing phase or a bad week. It’s not a \"belief\" that just popped into my head recently. It’s been nearly a year of him plotting and scheming right under my roof, day after day, week after week. Nine months of me having to double-check my own toolboxes and watch my back in my own kitchen. So yeah, it's been a lot longer than a month, I can promise you that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there is any implausible or impossible explanation for these events, or is it only that this one person is secretly messing with your things?\n\nPatient: Implausible? Like... what, like ghosts or aliens or the CIA or something? No, no, Doc, let's keep our feet on the ground here. I'm a practical man. I deal with steel and rivets, not science fiction. There's nothing impossible about it, and that's exactly why it's so incredibly frustrating that I'm sitting here. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I'm just making up fairy tales in my head. But I know—I absolutely know—that my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. \n\nIt's just him. Just a regular guy with a mean streak and too much time on his hands. I mean, if you just look at the facts rationally, my tools don’t move themselves. I've had the exact same organizational system in my garage for twenty years. My socket wrenches go in the top drawer of the red Craftsman chest, the crescent wrenches in the second, organized by size from left to right. You don't just wake up one morning and find the 3/8 inch drive sitting over in the drill bit tray by magic. Somebody physically walked in there, picked it up, and moved it just to mess with my head. Him. \n\nAnd it’s not just the garage, it's everything I try to do to find a little peace and quiet. Take my fishing plans. Spoiled, almost every single weekend. I'll get my tackle box ready on a Friday night, check the lines, make sure I've got my lures all set up for bass down at the reservoir, and come Saturday morning at 4:30 AM, suddenly the latch on the box is jammed shut with superglue, or my good spinning reel is mysteriously missing the handle. By accident? Come on. You don't have accidents like that every single weekend for nine straight months. \n\nHe's doing it on purpose, and there's no crazy sci-fi explanation for it. After all these months of watching him operate, I can see plain as day what his end game is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. He wants to make me look like a raving lunatic to my wife so she'll finally throw her hands up and say, \"That's it, he's lost his marbles,\" and kick me to the curb. And then what happens? He gets the run of the place. He gets the house, he gets my garage, he gets the comfortable life I’ve spent thirty years breaking my back to build. There's nothing impossible about it, Doc. It's just plain, old-fashioned sabotage.\n\nDoctor: During the times when you felt this was happening, did you also feel unusually sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: Sad? Depressed? Well... no. No, I wouldn't use those words at all. I mean, sure, I get angry. I get frustrated. Anybody would get frustrated if they were dealing with what I'm dealing with. You work hard all week, you just want to come home and relax, maybe head out to the reservoir on a Saturday morning, and instead you're spending three hours trying to un-jam a superglued latch on your tackle box. You don't get depressed when you're in a war, Doc. You get mad, and you get focused. And that's exactly what I am. I'm focused on protecting what's mine. I don't have time for depression.\n\nDoctor: If I were to show you clear evidence that no one has been entering the house or moving your tools, would that change your mind?\n\nPatient: Clear evidence? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hearing or seeing things? Like... what, like voices in my head? Or floating shadows? No. No, Doc, come on now. I told you, I'm a practical guy. I don't have an overactive imagination. I've worked on a fabrication floor for over thirty years. You think I’d still have all ten of my fingers if I was seeing things that weren't there? When you're running a heavy-duty band saw or a drill press, you need perfect vision, and you need to hear the exact pitch of the motor to know if the blade's catching on the steel. My senses are sharp. They have to be. I get my hearing checked every year for work, and it's fine. Better than fine.

So no, I'm not hearing things that other people can't hear. Well... I mean, I hear things my *wife* claims she doesn't hear, but that's just because she's a heavy sleeper and she's got her head buried in the sand about her family. Like the floorboards. We've got this oak hardwood in the hallway—I put it in myself back in '06, beautiful wood, but there's a specific squeak right near the air return vent by the guest room. I lay there in bed, wide awake, and I hear that squeak at 2:00, maybe 2:30 in the morning. 

That's not a hallucination, Doc. That's not me hearing voices. That is the very real, physical sound of a 180-pound freeloader sneaking out of his room and heading down to my garage to mess with my gear. My wife will wake up, see me listening, and say, "Oh, it's just the house settling, you're hearing things." The house settling! Give me a break. A house doesn't settle and then mysteriously leave superglue on my tackle box. 

So, no. No imaginary voices. No visions. I don't need to make things up when the reality of what he's doing is right in front of my face. It's just my own two eyes and ears telling me exactly what that guy is up to, plain as day, while everybody else just pretends it isn't happening.

I know for an absolute fact that my wife’s relative has been coming into this house just to get at me. \n\nI mean, I work as a fabricator, right? But I'll go out to my garage, and my things are shifted around. My tools don’t just move themselves. A socket wrench doesn't just sprout legs and walk across the workbench, you know? But everyone just says, \"Oh, you just misplaced it, you're getting older.\" No, I didn't misplace a thing. \n\nAnd it’s not just the garage, either. Every single weekend—and I mean every weekend without fail—my fishing plans get completely spoiled by some \"accident.\" My gear is messed with, the line is tangled up, or something is suddenly wrong with the truck. That is not a coincidence. After all these months of this happening, day in and day out, I can see plain as day what's going on. He’s been trying to take over my place. He wants to push me out of my own home, just edge me right out of the picture entirely, and nobody wants to listen to me about it. They just sit there and tell me I'm crazy, but I'm the one living it.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that this is really happening, if at all?\n\nPatient: How certain am I? Doc, I am one hundred percent, absolutely, unequivocally certain. There is not a single shadow of a doubt in my mind. I mean, look at it this way. If you walked into your office here, right, and your pens were moved from the left side of your desk to the right, and your chair was lowered three inches, and your files were out of alphabetical order, would you just say, 'Oh, well, I guess I'm just losing my mind'? No, you'd know someone had been in here. \n\nThat's exactly what I'm dealing with. I have been an assembler for thirty-two years. I don't accidentally leave the chuck key out of the drill press. But I go out there, and it's moved. And my wife's relative—he was over here for Thanksgiving, right? And ever since then, it's been this slow, methodical campaign. \n\nIt’s not just the garage, like I said. It’s the fishing gear. Every Saturday morning, I wake up at 4:30 AM. I make my coffee—black, no sugar—and I go out to load the truck. And every single time, without fail, something is wrong. Last week, the spark plug wires on my outboard motor were swapped. Swapped! You don't just accidentally swap spark plug wires. Two weeks before that, someone had let exactly ten pounds of air out of my front left tire. Just enough to be a problem, not enough to be obvious right away. \n\nAnd my wife, she just sighs and rolls her eyes and tells me I'm being paranoid. But I see what he's doing. It's so clear to me. He wants to make me look incompetent. He wants her to think I'm losing it, so he can step in, be the man of the house, and push me right out the door. I’ve seen the way he looks around my property, sizing it up. I know it sounds crazy to you, and I know it sounds crazy to my family, but I am living it every single day. I am as certain of this as I am that the sun is going to come up tomorrow morning. There is no 'if at all' about it. It is happening.\n\nDoctor: Do any other people in your family or community believe this is happening too?\n\nPatient: Not a single one of them, Doc. And that... well, that right there is exactly why I’m sitting in this chair talking to you today. I’m only here because everybody—and I mean everybody, my wife, my kids, the neighbors—keeps acting like I’m imagining the whole thing. It’s like talking to a brick wall. I try to sit my wife down, right? We'll be at the kitchen table, and I’ll say, 'Look at this. Look at how the workbench was left.' And she just sighs, gets that pitying look on her face, and tells me to let it go. Like I'm a child making up ghost stories in the dark. \n\nBut I know what I know. I know my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. It's not a theory, it's a fact. But they all just gang up and say, 'Oh, you're working too hard, you're just stressed out from the shop.' Stressed? Does stress pick up a heavy-duty torque wrench and move it to the opposite side of the garage? Because my tools don’t move themselves! I’ve been working with my hands for thirty-two years, I know how gravity works. \n\nAnd I try to tell them about the weekends, too. I lay it all out for them, completely logically. I explain that my fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. Once? Sure, maybe you get a tangled line. Twice? Bad luck. But every weekend for months? You'd think after the fifth or sixth time the truck gets messed with, someone in my family would say, 'Hey, maybe he's onto something.' But no. They just look at me like I'm the one losing my grip. \n\nIt’s isolating, Doc, I’ll tell you that much. You work your whole life, providing for your family, keeping your house in perfect order, and then this happens. After all these months of this going on, I can see plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place. He wants to push me out. He plays the nice guy to them, smiling and making small talk, and meanwhile, he's methodically trying to erase me from my own life so he can step right into my shoes. My hearing is fine, my vision is fine. \n\nWhat I see is exactly what is there in the physical world. Real, tangible things. When I go out to the garage, I'm not imagining that my 5/8 inch wrench is sitting on the wrong shelf. It is physically sitting there. Anyone could see it if they just bothered to actually look! If my wife walked out there and actually looked at the pegboard instead of just rolling her eyes, she would see it too. But she doesn't. \n\nIn my line of work—fabrication, assembling—you have to trust your eyes and your ears. If a machine has a bad bearing, you hear the rattle. It's a real sound. If a piece of sheet metal is cut a sixteenth of an inch off, you see it. You don't imagine it, it's just a fact of the material. And that's how I operate. I deal in hard facts. So when I look under the hood of my truck and see the spark plug wires swapped, I am looking at real rubber and real wire. I can touch them. I definitely don't have any extra energy to spare, I can promise you that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever used methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other recreational drugs or new medications around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Methamphetamine? Cocaine? Doc, I... I don't even know what to say to that. Look, I don't know what kind of people usually sit in that chair across from you, but you have definitely got the wrong guy. I am a hardworking, tax-paying man. I've been at the same fabrication shop for over thirty years. \n\nDo you have any idea what would happen if someone showed up to a metalworking shop hopped up on meth or cocaine? You'd lose an arm. You'd get someone else killed. We are operating heavy hydraulic presses, we're using shears that can slice right through half-inch steel plate like it's warm butter. You have to be sharp. You have to be completely focused. The shop foreman, Gary, he runs random drug tests every single quarter, and I have never failed one in my entire life. I've never even smoked marijuana. Not even back in the eighties when half the guys on the floor were doing it. It's just not who I am. I might have a cold beer on the porch on a Friday evening, but that is the absolute extent of it. \n\nAnd as for new medications, no, absolutely not. I don't like taking pills. I take a standard multivitamin that my wife picks up at the grocery store, and if my lower back is really screaming at me after a long shift hunched over the welding table, I might take a couple of ibuprofen. That's it. My regular doctor hasn't prescribed me anything new, I haven't started taking any weird supplements or anything like that. \n\nSo if you're asking this because you think I'm hallucinating all this stuff because I'm on drugs, you are barking way up the wrong tree. My head is completely clear. That's exactly what I keep trying to explain to everybody. I am completely sober, completely rational, and completely aware of my surroundings. It's honestly a little insulting that my family drags me in here, treating me like I'm crazy, and now I'm having to defend myself against being some kind of drug addict, all just because I'm the only one who actually pays attention to what's happening in my own house. I don't need drugs to see that my tools have been messed with. I just need my own two eyes.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs been present for at least one month?\n\nPatient: One month? Doc, if this had only been going on for a month, I probably wouldn't even be sitting here right now because I'd still be trying to give the guy the benefit of the doubt. I'd still be telling myself, \"Oh, maybe I really did just misplace that wrench,\" or \"Maybe the shed door just blew open in the wind.\" \n\nNo, no, it has been way, way longer than a month. We are talking about nine months. Nine solid months of this... this absolute nightmare. \n\nIt started exactly—and I mean exactly—when my wife's relative moved into our home. Before he dragged his suitcases through my front door, my life was completely normal. I had peace. I'd come home from the fabrication shop, have my dinner, head out to the garage to tinker with my gear, and everything was right where I left it. My house was my castle, just like they say. But for the last nine months? Every single day is a test. Every single day I have to wake up wondering what he's going to sabotage next, how he's going to try and make me look like a fool in front of my own wife. \n\nYou try living on high alert in your own living room for nine months, Doc. It wears a man down. It’s not just some passing phase or a bad week. It’s not a \"belief\" that just popped into my head recently. It’s been nearly a year of him plotting and scheming right under my roof, day after day, week after week. Nine months of me having to double-check my own toolboxes and watch my back in my own kitchen. So yeah, it's been a lot longer than a month, I can promise you that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there is any implausible or impossible explanation for these events, or is it only that this one person is secretly messing with your things?\n\nPatient: Implausible? Like... what, like ghosts or aliens or the CIA or something? No, no, Doc, let's keep our feet on the ground here. I'm a practical man. I deal with steel and rivets, not science fiction. There's nothing impossible about it, and that's exactly why it's so incredibly frustrating that I'm sitting here. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I'm just making up fairy tales in my head. But I know—I absolutely know—that my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. \n\nIt's just him. Just a regular guy with a mean streak and too much time on his hands. I mean, if you just look at the facts rationally, my tools don’t move themselves. I've had the exact same organizational system in my garage for twenty years. My socket wrenches go in the top drawer of the red Craftsman chest, the crescent wrenches in the second, organized by size from left to right. You don't just wake up one morning and find the 3/8 inch drive sitting over in the drill bit tray by magic. Somebody physically walked in there, picked it up, and moved it just to mess with my head. Him. \n\nAnd it’s not just the garage, it's everything I try to do to find a little peace and quiet. Take my fishing plans. Spoiled, almost every single weekend. I'll get my tackle box ready on a Friday night, check the lines, make sure I've got my lures all set up for bass down at the reservoir, and come Saturday morning at 4:30 AM, suddenly the latch on the box is jammed shut with superglue, or my good spinning reel is mysteriously missing the handle. By accident? Come on. You don't have accidents like that every single weekend for nine straight months. \n\nHe's doing it on purpose, and there's no crazy sci-fi explanation for it. After all these months of watching him operate, I can see plain as day what his end game is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. He wants to make me look like a raving lunatic to my wife so she'll finally throw her hands up and say, \"That's it, he's lost his marbles,\" and kick me to the curb. And then what happens? He gets the run of the place. He gets the house, he gets my garage, he gets the comfortable life I’ve spent thirty years breaking my back to build. There's nothing impossible about it, Doc. It's just plain, old-fashioned sabotage.\n\nDoctor: During the times when you felt this was happening, did you also feel unusually sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: Sad? Depressed? Well... no. No, I wouldn't use those words at all. I mean, sure, I get angry. I get frustrated. Anybody would get frustrated if they were dealing with what I'm dealing with. You work hard all week, you just want to come home and relax, maybe head out to the reservoir on a Saturday morning, and instead you're spending three hours trying to un-jam a superglued latch on your tackle box. That makes a man mad, Doc. It makes you want to put your fist through a wall. \n\nBut depressed? Like... like sitting around crying or not wanting to get out of bed? No, sir. Absolutely not. If anything, this whole situation has kept me on my toes. I have to get out of bed. I have to be alert. If I just lay around moping, who knows what he'd do next? He'd probably take a hacksaw to the brake lines on my truck while I was feeling sorry for myself. \n\nSo no, I haven't been sad. I don't have the time or the luxury to be sad. I've been busy. Busy trying to catch him in the act, busy trying to fix the things he breaks, busy trying to explain to my wife—who, by the way, just rolls her eyes at me half the time now—that this guy is systematically trying to ruin my life. You don't get depressed when you're in a war, Doc. You get mad, and you get focused. And that's exactly what I am. I'm focused on protecting what's mine. I don't have time for depression.\n\nDoctor: If I were to show you clear evidence that no one has been entering the house or moving your tools, would that change your mind?\n\nPatient: Clear evidence? What kind of evidence are we even talking about here? Cameras? Because let me tell you, Doc, if you put up a camera in the garage, he’s just going to find the blind spot. He’s sneaky like that. And you say evidence of 'no one entering the house'—well, he already lives there! He’s in the guest room right down the hall from the kitchen, so he doesn't need to break in. He doesn't need to pick a lock. He just waits until I'm dead asleep. \n\nLook, this... this right here is exactly why I’m sitting in this chair. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My wife, her family, and now you're talking about showing me hypothetical 'evidence' to try and prove that I'm just seeing things. But I know—I absolutely know without a single shadow of a doubt—that my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. \n\nYou can show me whatever paperwork or video or whatever it is you think you have, but it doesn't change the basic laws of physics. My tools don’t move themselves. They just don't. I've worked in fabrication my whole life, I know exactly where I put my gear, down to the millimeter. And my fishing plans? They don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. A latch doesn't just accidentally glue itself shut on a Saturday morning. A reel doesn't unscrew its own handle and walk away. It takes a person to do that. \n\nAnd after all these months of dealing with this, of watching him operate, of seeing the little smirk on his face when my wife tells me I'm being paranoid... I can see plain as day what his plan is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. That's the bottom line. He wants me gone so he can have my life, my house, my garage. So no, Doc, no 'evidence' is going to change my mind, because I'm the one living in the middle of this warzone, and I know exactly who the enemy is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences of hearing or seeing things that other people around you could not?\n\nPatient: Hearing or seeing things? Like... what, like voices in my head? Or floating shadows? No. No, Doc, come on now. I told you, I'm a practical guy. I don't have an overactive imagination. I've worked on a fabrication floor for over thirty years. You think I’d still have all ten of my fingers if I was seeing things that weren't there? When you're running a heavy-duty band saw or a drill press, you need perfect vision, and you need to hear the exact pitch of the motor to know if the blade's catching on the steel. My senses are sharp. They have to be. I get my hearing checked every year for work, and it's fine. Better than fine.\n\nSo no, I'm not hearing things that other people can't hear. Well... I mean, I hear things my *wife* claims she doesn't hear, but that's just because she's a heavy sleeper and she's got her head buried in the sand about her family. Like the floorboards. We've got this oak hardwood in the hallway—I put it in myself back in '06, beautiful wood, but there's a specific squeak right near the air return vent by the guest room. I lay there in bed, wide awake, and I hear that squeak at 2:00, maybe 2:30 in the morning. \n\nThat's not a hallucination, Doc. That's not me hearing voices. That is the very real, physical sound of a 180-pound freeloader sneaking out of his room and heading down to my garage to mess with my gear. My wife will wake up, see me listening, and say, \"Oh, it's just the house settling, you're hearing things.\" The house settling! Give me a break. A house doesn't settle and then mysteriously leave superglue on my tackle box. \n\nSo, no. No imaginary voices. No visions. I don't need to make things up when the reality of what he's doing is right in front of my face. It's just my own two eyes and ears telling me exactly what that guy is up to, plain as day, while everybody else just pretends it isn't happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

  }

Mistaken? Doc... I feel like a broken record here. We literally just went over this. My wife, her family, and now you, asking if I'm 'mistaken.' 

How could I possibly be mistaken? I know for an absolute fact that my wife’s relative came into this house to get at me. You don't just 'mistakenly' think someone is sabotaging your life when you're the one cleaning up the mess every single day. I mean, let's just look at the facts, alright? Just the pure, hard facts. Suddenly, the half-inch drive is sitting on the completely opposite side of the garage, tucked behind a bucket of joint compound? Tools don't grow legs, Doc. They just don't. 

And the fishing... I mean, come on. My fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. It’s a statistical impossibility! One week it's the superglued latch, the next week someone has taken a pair of wire cutters to my best braided line, the week after that my favorite lures are just gone. Vanished. You string all those 'accidents' together, week after week, month after month, and it's not a mistake. It's a campaign. 

I'm not blind, and I'm not stupid. After all these months of watching him skulk around, I can see plain as day what his endgame is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. That’s what this whole thing is about. He figures if he drives me crazy enough, if he gets my wife to think I'm losing my marbles by complaining about things she refuses to see, then I'm the one who gets kicked out, or locked up, or whatever. And then what happens? He gets the run of the house. He gets my garage. He gets my life. 

So, no. There is zero chance I'm mistaken. I wish I was, Doc, I really do. It would be a heck of a lot easier to just be some forgetful old guy who misplaces his wrenches. But I'm not forgetful, and I'm not mistaken. I know exactly what he's doing.

Every single weekend—and I mean every weekend without fail—my fishing plans get completely spoiled by some \"accident.\" My gear is messed with, the line is tangled up, or something is suddenly wrong with the truck. That is not a coincidence. After all these months of this happening, day in and day out, I can see plain as day what's going on. He’s been trying to take over my place. He wants to push me out of my own home, just edge me right out of the picture entirely, and nobody wants to listen to me about it. They just sit there and tell me I'm crazy, but I'm the one living it.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that this is really happening, if at all?\n\nPatient: How certain am I? Doc, I am one hundred percent, absolutely, unequivocally certain. There is not a single shadow of a doubt in my mind. I mean, look at it this way. If you walked into your office here, right, and your pens were moved from the left side of your desk to the right, and your chair was lowered three inches, and your files were out of alphabetical order, would you just say, 'Oh, well, I guess I'm just losing my mind'? No, you'd know someone had been in here. \n\nThat's exactly what I'm dealing with. I have been an assembler for thirty-two years. Thirty-two years of putting things together, exactly where they belong, piece by piece. My brain works like a blueprint. I don't misplace a 5/8 inch wrench. I don't accidentally leave the chuck key out of the drill press. But I go out there, and it's moved. And my wife's relative—he was over here for Thanksgiving, right? And ever since then, it's been this slow, methodical campaign. \n\nIt’s not just the garage, like I said. It’s the fishing gear. Every Saturday morning, I wake up at 4:30 AM. I make my coffee—black, no sugar—and I go out to load the truck. And every single time, without fail, something is wrong. Last week, the spark plug wires on my outboard motor were swapped. Swapped! You don't just accidentally swap spark plug wires. Two weeks before that, someone had let exactly ten pounds of air out of my front left tire. Just enough to be a problem, not enough to be obvious right away. \n\nAnd my wife, she just sighs and rolls her eyes and tells me I'm being paranoid. But I see what he's doing. It's so clear to me. He wants to make me look incompetent. He wants her to think I'm losing it, so he can step in, be the man of the house, and push me right out the door. I’ve seen the way he looks around my property, sizing it up. I know it sounds crazy to you, and I know it sounds crazy to my family, but I am living it every single day. I am as certain of this as I am that the sun is going to come up tomorrow morning. There is no 'if at all' about it. It is happening.\n\nDoctor: Do any other people in your family or community believe this is happening too?\n\nPatient: Not a single one of them, Doc. And that... well, that right there is exactly why I’m sitting in this chair talking to you today. I’m only here because everybody—and I mean everybody, my wife, my kids, the neighbors—keeps acting like I’m imagining the whole thing. It’s like talking to a brick wall. I try to sit my wife down, right? We'll be at the kitchen table, and I’ll say, 'Look at this. Look at how the workbench was left.' And she just sighs, gets that pitying look on her face, and tells me to let it go. Like I'm a child making up ghost stories in the dark. \n\nBut I know what I know. I know my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. It's not a theory, it's a fact. But they all just gang up and say, 'Oh, you're working too hard, you're just stressed out from the shop.' Stressed? Does stress pick up a heavy-duty torque wrench and move it to the opposite side of the garage? Because my tools don’t move themselves! I’ve been working with my hands for thirty-two years, I know how gravity works. \n\nAnd I try to tell them about the weekends, too. I lay it all out for them, completely logically. I explain that my fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. Once? Sure, maybe you get a tangled line. Twice? Bad luck. But every weekend for months? You'd think after the fifth or sixth time the truck gets messed with, someone in my family would say, 'Hey, maybe he's onto something.' But no. They just look at me like I'm the one losing my grip. \n\nIt’s isolating, Doc, I’ll tell you that much. You work your whole life, providing for your family, keeping your house in perfect order, and then this happens. After all these months of this going on, I can see plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place. He wants to push me out. He plays the nice guy to them, smiling and making small talk, and meanwhile, he's methodically trying to erase me from my own life so he can step right into my shoes. And nobody—not one single person—will just open their eyes and see what's happening right in front of them.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard or seen things that other people around you do not?\n\nPatient: No, no, no. Doc, I think you're misunderstanding me. I'm not... I don't hear voices whispering in the walls, and I'm not seeing ghosts or shadows or anything like that. I know exactly what you're getting at with a question like that, and let me tell you, my mind is not playing tricks on me. My hearing is fine, my vision is fine. \n\nWhat I see is exactly what is there in the physical world. Real, tangible things. When I go out to the garage, I'm not imagining that my 5/8 inch wrench is sitting on the wrong shelf. It is physically sitting there. Anyone could see it if they just bothered to actually look! If my wife walked out there and actually looked at the pegboard instead of just rolling her eyes, she would see it too. But she doesn't. \n\nIn my line of work—fabrication, assembling—you have to trust your eyes and your ears. If a machine has a bad bearing, you hear the rattle. It's a real sound. If a piece of sheet metal is cut a sixteenth of an inch off, you see it. You don't imagine it, it's just a fact of the material. And that's how I operate. I deal in hard facts. So when I look under the hood of my truck and see the spark plug wires swapped, I am looking at real rubber and real wire. I can touch them. I can hold them in my hands. \n\nI don't hear things other people don't hear. I hear my wife sighing at me. I hear the neighbor's dog barking. I hear real things. And I see real things. I see that relative of hers sizing up my living room. I see the tire on my truck sitting a little lower because exactly ten pounds of air is gone. It’s all real, physical evidence, Doc. So no, I'm not making things up in my head. Everything I'm dealing with is happening right here in the real world, in broad daylight. It's just that I'm the only one willing to actually open my eyes and look at the evidence.\n\nDoctor: Besides this one person, do you believe anyone else is targeting you or trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's not... look, I'm not one of those guys who thinks the CIA is tapping my phone or the mailman is poisoning my water. I'm a rational man. I don't need to invent some giant, crazy conspiracy with a whole army of people out to get me when the truth is sitting right there in my own living room. It's just him. Just my wife's relative. He doesn't need anyone else helping him because he’s sneaky enough on his own, playing the long game. \n\nAnd I know I keep coming back to this, Doc, and I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record here, but you have to understand—I’m only sitting in this office because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My whole family is treating me like I'm the one with the problem. But I know what I know. I know my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. He doesn't need accomplices to sneak into my garage. Like I said, my tools don’t move themselves. A heavy-duty wrench doesn't just sprout legs and walk to a different workbench. My fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. It's a targeted, one-man operation. \n\nWhy would he involve anybody else? That would just mean sharing the prize, right? And after all these months of dealing with this, day in and day out, I can see plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. He wants my life. He wants to be the one sitting at the head of my dinner table, being the man of the house. He wants my wife to look at him like he's the capable one, the one who doesn't 'misplace' his tools or mess up the outboard motor. \n\nSo, no. It's not the neighbors. It's not the guys down at the fabrication shop, they're good guys, we've worked together for years. It is just him. He is the one doing it, and he's doing it right under everyone's noses, smiling at my wife while he takes a sledgehammer to my life, and it just drives me up the wall that nobody else will open their eyes and see it.\n\nDoctor: Have you also been having periods of feeling unusually energized or needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Energized? No, no, I don't know where you'd get an idea like that, Doc. If anything, I'm just flat-out exhausted from having to deal with all this nonsense day in and day out. \n\nI mean, look, I'm a working man. And let me tell you, when you spend eight, sometimes ten hours a day on your feet, working with heavy shears and presses and wrestling with sheet metal... by the time you get home, your bones are aching and you are just beat. \n\nSo no, I'm not bouncing off the walls or staying up all night doing jumping jacks. I need my sleep. You can't be sleep-deprived around industrial equipment, or you'll end up losing a finger, or worse. I usually try to be in bed, boots off, lights out, by 10:00 PM sharp. \n\nNow, do I always fall right to sleep the second my head hits the pillow? Well, no, of course not. Sometimes I'll lie there staring at the ceiling for a while, but that's just because my mind is turning over the facts. I'll be trying to figure out how he managed to bypass the padlock on the shed without leaving a scratch, or I'll be running through a mental checklist of what I need to double-check and secure the next morning so my weekend doesn't get ruined again. \n\nBut that's not... that's not having \"too much energy\" or needing less sleep. That's just normal, rational worry. That's just the reality of having to constantly be on guard in your own home because someone is actively trying to dismantle your life piece by piece while everyone else looks the other way. I want to sleep. I want to just close my eyes and rest like a normal guy. I definitely don't have any extra energy to spare, I can promise you that much.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever used methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other recreational drugs or new medications around the time this started?\n\nPatient: Methamphetamine? Cocaine? Doc, I... I don't even know what to say to that. Look, I don't know what kind of people usually sit in that chair across from you, but you have definitely got the wrong guy. I am a hardworking, tax-paying man. I've been at the same fabrication shop for over thirty years. \n\nDo you have any idea what would happen if someone showed up to a metalworking shop hopped up on meth or cocaine? You'd lose an arm. You'd get someone else killed. We are operating heavy hydraulic presses, we're using shears that can slice right through half-inch steel plate like it's warm butter. You have to be sharp. You have to be completely focused. The shop foreman, Gary, he runs random drug tests every single quarter, and I have never failed one in my entire life. I've never even smoked marijuana. Not even back in the eighties when half the guys on the floor were doing it. It's just not who I am. I might have a cold beer on the porch on a Friday evening, but that is the absolute extent of it. \n\nAnd as for new medications, no, absolutely not. I don't like taking pills. I take a standard multivitamin that my wife picks up at the grocery store, and if my lower back is really screaming at me after a long shift hunched over the welding table, I might take a couple of ibuprofen. That's it. My regular doctor hasn't prescribed me anything new, I haven't started taking any weird supplements or anything like that. \n\nSo if you're asking this because you think I'm hallucinating all this stuff because I'm on drugs, you are barking way up the wrong tree. I don't need drugs to see that my tools have been messed with. I just need my own two eyes.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs been present for at least one month?\n\nPatient: One month? Doc, if this had only been going on for a month, I probably wouldn't even be sitting here right now because I'd still be trying to give the guy the benefit of the doubt. By accident? Come on. You don't have accidents like that every single weekend for nine straight months. \n\nHe's doing it on purpose, and there's no crazy sci-fi explanation for it. He wants to make me look like a raving lunatic to my wife so she'll finally throw her hands up and say, \"That's it, he's lost his marbles,\" and kick me to the curb. And then what happens? He gets the run of the place. He gets the house, he gets my garage, he gets the comfortable life I’ve spent thirty years breaking my back to build. There's nothing impossible about it, Doc. It's just plain, old-fashioned sabotage.\n\nDoctor: During the times when you felt this was happening, did you also feel unusually sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: Sad? Depressed? Well... no. No, I wouldn't use those words at all. I mean, sure, I get angry. I get frustrated. Anybody would get frustrated if they were dealing with what I'm dealing with. You work hard all week, you just want to come home and relax, maybe head out to the reservoir on a Saturday morning, and instead you're spending three hours trying to un-jam a superglued latch on your tackle box. Like... what, like voices in my head? Or floating shadows? No. No, Doc, come on now. I told you, I'm a practical guy. I don't have an overactive imagination. I've worked on a fabrication floor for over thirty years. You think I’d still have all ten of my fingers if I was seeing things that weren't there? When you're running a heavy-duty band saw or a drill press, you need perfect vision, and you need to hear the exact pitch of the motor to know if the blade's catching on the steel. My senses are sharp. They have to be. I get my hearing checked every year for work, and it's fine. Better than fine.\n\nSo no, I'm not hearing things that other people can't hear. Well... I lay there in bed, wide awake, and I hear that squeak at 2:00, maybe 2:30 in the morning. \n\nThat's not a hallucination, Doc. That's not me hearing voices. That is the very real, physical sound of a 180-pound freeloader sneaking out of his room and heading down to my garage to mess with my gear. I'm sitting in this chair, taking time out of my day—time I could be using to re-inventory the workbench he messed with—specifically because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My wife, her family, and now you, asking if I'm 'mistaken.' \n\nHow could I possibly be mistaken? I know for an absolute fact that my wife’s relative came into this house to get at me. My tools don’t move themselves. I've got a Snap-on socket set I bought back in '98. I keep it in the exact same drawer, organized by size, and I wipe them down after every use. After all these months of watching him skulk around, I can see plain as day what his endgame is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. That’s what this whole thing is about. He figures if he drives me crazy enough, if he gets my wife to think I'm losing my marbles by complaining about things she refuses to see, then I'm the one who gets kicked out, or locked up, or whatever. And then what happens? He gets the run of the house. He gets my garage. He gets my life. \n\nSo, no. There is zero chance I'm mistaken. I wish I was, Doc, I really do. It would be a heck of a lot easier to just be some forgetful old guy who misplaces his wrenches. But I'm not forgetful, and I'm not mistaken. I know exactly what he's doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Anyone else? No, no, no. Why would there be anyone else? It’s not some big, crazy conspiracy with the government or the neighborhood watch or the mailman. I’m not crazy, Doc. He’s the one sneaking around with the superglue and the wire cutters. 

I’ve spent months—months, Doc—just observing this whole situation, keeping my mouth shut, taking mental notes of every little thing he does. And after all these months, I can see plain as day what his game is. He doesn't need anyone else's help because his plan is actually pretty simple. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. That’s it. That’s the whole grand scheme. He pushes me out, he gets my wife to think I've lost it, and then he gets to sit in my recliner, park in my garage, and use the tools I paid for with thirty years of sweat on a fabrication floor. 

So no, there’s nobody else. Just one greedy, manipulative guy who wants what I built. And I'm not going to just roll over and let him have it, no matter how many doctors my wife makes me talk to.

Every single day I have to wake up wondering what he's going to sabotage next, how he's going to try and make me look like a fool in front of my own wife. \n\nYou try living on high alert in your own living room for nine months, Doc. It wears a man down. It’s not just some passing phase or a bad week. It’s not a \"belief\" that just popped into my head recently. It’s been nearly a year of him plotting and scheming right under my roof, day after day, week after week. Nine months of me having to double-check my own toolboxes and watch my back in my own kitchen. So yeah, it's been a lot longer than a month, I can promise you that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there is any implausible or impossible explanation for these events, or is it only that this one person is secretly messing with your things?\n\nPatient: Implausible? Like... what, like ghosts or aliens or the CIA or something? No, no, Doc, let's keep our feet on the ground here. I'm a practical man. I deal with steel and rivets, not science fiction. There's nothing impossible about it, and that's exactly why it's so incredibly frustrating that I'm sitting here. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it, like I'm just making up fairy tales in my head. But I know—I absolutely know—that my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. \n\nIt's just him. Just a regular guy with a mean streak and too much time on his hands. I mean, if you just look at the facts rationally, my tools don’t move themselves. I've had the exact same organizational system in my garage for twenty years. My socket wrenches go in the top drawer of the red Craftsman chest, the crescent wrenches in the second, organized by size from left to right. You don't just wake up one morning and find the 3/8 inch drive sitting over in the drill bit tray by magic. Somebody physically walked in there, picked it up, and moved it just to mess with my head. Him. \n\nAnd it’s not just the garage, it's everything I try to do to find a little peace and quiet. Take my fishing plans. Spoiled, almost every single weekend. I'll get my tackle box ready on a Friday night, check the lines, make sure I've got my lures all set up for bass down at the reservoir, and come Saturday morning at 4:30 AM, suddenly the latch on the box is jammed shut with superglue, or my good spinning reel is mysteriously missing the handle. By accident? Come on. You don't have accidents like that every single weekend for nine straight months. \n\nHe's doing it on purpose, and there's no crazy sci-fi explanation for it. After all these months of watching him operate, I can see plain as day what his end game is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. He wants to make me look like a raving lunatic to my wife so she'll finally throw her hands up and say, \"That's it, he's lost his marbles,\" and kick me to the curb. And then what happens? He gets the run of the place. He gets the house, he gets my garage, he gets the comfortable life I’ve spent thirty years breaking my back to build. There's nothing impossible about it, Doc. It's just plain, old-fashioned sabotage.\n\nDoctor: During the times when you felt this was happening, did you also feel unusually sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: Sad? Depressed? Well... no. No, I wouldn't use those words at all. I mean, sure, I get angry. I get frustrated. Anybody would get frustrated if they were dealing with what I'm dealing with. You work hard all week, you just want to come home and relax, maybe head out to the reservoir on a Saturday morning, and instead you're spending three hours trying to un-jam a superglued latch on your tackle box. That makes a man mad, Doc. It makes you want to put your fist through a wall. \n\nBut depressed? Like... like sitting around crying or not wanting to get out of bed? No, sir. Absolutely not. If anything, this whole situation has kept me on my toes. I have to get out of bed. I have to be alert. If I just lay around moping, who knows what he'd do next? He'd probably take a hacksaw to the brake lines on my truck while I was feeling sorry for myself. \n\nSo no, I haven't been sad. I don't have the time or the luxury to be sad. I've been busy. Busy trying to catch him in the act, busy trying to fix the things he breaks, busy trying to explain to my wife—who, by the way, just rolls her eyes at me half the time now—that this guy is systematically trying to ruin my life. You don't get depressed when you're in a war, Doc. You get mad, and you get focused. And that's exactly what I am. I'm focused on protecting what's mine. I don't have time for depression.\n\nDoctor: If I were to show you clear evidence that no one has been entering the house or moving your tools, would that change your mind?\n\nPatient: Clear evidence? What kind of evidence are we even talking about here? Cameras? Because let me tell you, Doc, if you put up a camera in the garage, he’s just going to find the blind spot. He’s sneaky like that. And you say evidence of 'no one entering the house'—well, he already lives there! He’s in the guest room right down the hall from the kitchen, so he doesn't need to break in. He doesn't need to pick a lock. He just waits until I'm dead asleep. \n\nLook, this... this right here is exactly why I’m sitting in this chair. I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My wife, her family, and now you're talking about showing me hypothetical 'evidence' to try and prove that I'm just seeing things. But I know—I absolutely know without a single shadow of a doubt—that my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. \n\nYou can show me whatever paperwork or video or whatever it is you think you have, but it doesn't change the basic laws of physics. My tools don’t move themselves. They just don't. I've worked in fabrication my whole life, I know exactly where I put my gear, down to the millimeter. And my fishing plans? They don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. A latch doesn't just accidentally glue itself shut on a Saturday morning. A reel doesn't unscrew its own handle and walk away. It takes a person to do that. \n\nAnd after all these months of dealing with this, of watching him operate, of seeing the little smirk on his face when my wife tells me I'm being paranoid... I can see plain as day what his plan is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. That's the bottom line. He wants me gone so he can have my life, my house, my garage. So no, Doc, no 'evidence' is going to change my mind, because I'm the one living in the middle of this warzone, and I know exactly who the enemy is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences of hearing or seeing things that other people around you could not?\n\nPatient: Hearing or seeing things? Like... what, like voices in my head? Or floating shadows? No. No, Doc, come on now. I told you, I'm a practical guy. I don't have an overactive imagination. I've worked on a fabrication floor for over thirty years. You think I’d still have all ten of my fingers if I was seeing things that weren't there? When you're running a heavy-duty band saw or a drill press, you need perfect vision, and you need to hear the exact pitch of the motor to know if the blade's catching on the steel. My senses are sharp. They have to be. I get my hearing checked every year for work, and it's fine. Better than fine.\n\nSo no, I'm not hearing things that other people can't hear. Well... I mean, I hear things my *wife* claims she doesn't hear, but that's just because she's a heavy sleeper and she's got her head buried in the sand about her family. Like the floorboards. We've got this oak hardwood in the hallway—I put it in myself back in '06, beautiful wood, but there's a specific squeak right near the air return vent by the guest room. I lay there in bed, wide awake, and I hear that squeak at 2:00, maybe 2:30 in the morning. \n\nThat's not a hallucination, Doc. That's not me hearing voices. That is the very real, physical sound of a 180-pound freeloader sneaking out of his room and heading down to my garage to mess with my gear. My wife will wake up, see me listening, and say, \"Oh, it's just the house settling, you're hearing things.\" The house settling! Give me a break. A house doesn't settle and then mysteriously leave superglue on my tackle box. \n\nSo, no. No imaginary voices. No visions. I don't need to make things up when the reality of what he's doing is right in front of my face. It's just my own two eyes and ears telling me exactly what that guy is up to, plain as day, while everybody else just pretends it isn't happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any chance at all that you could be mistaken about your wife's relative causing this?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? Doc... I feel like a broken record here. We literally just went over this. I'm sitting in this chair, taking time out of my day—time I could be using to re-inventory the workbench he messed with—specifically because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My wife, her family, and now you, asking if I'm 'mistaken.' \n\nHow could I possibly be mistaken? I know for an absolute fact that my wife’s relative came into this house to get at me. You don't just 'mistakenly' think someone is sabotaging your life when you're the one cleaning up the mess every single day. I mean, let's just look at the facts, alright? Just the pure, hard facts. My tools don’t move themselves. I've got a Snap-on socket set I bought back in '98. I keep it in the exact same drawer, organized by size, and I wipe them down after every use. Suddenly, the half-inch drive is sitting on the completely opposite side of the garage, tucked behind a bucket of joint compound? Tools don't grow legs, Doc. They just don't. \n\nAnd the fishing... I mean, come on. My fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. It’s a statistical impossibility! One week it's the superglued latch, the next week someone has taken a pair of wire cutters to my best braided line, the week after that my favorite lures are just gone. Vanished. You string all those 'accidents' together, week after week, month after month, and it's not a mistake. It's a campaign. \n\nI'm not blind, and I'm not stupid. After all these months of watching him skulk around, I can see plain as day what his endgame is. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. That’s what this whole thing is about. He figures if he drives me crazy enough, if he gets my wife to think I'm losing my marbles by complaining about things she refuses to see, then I'm the one who gets kicked out, or locked up, or whatever. And then what happens? He gets the run of the house. He gets my garage. He gets my life. \n\nSo, no. There is zero chance I'm mistaken. I wish I was, Doc, I really do. It would be a heck of a lot easier to just be some forgetful old guy who misplaces his wrenches. But I'm not forgetful, and I'm not mistaken. I know exactly what he's doing.\n\nDoctor: Besides your wife’s relative, do you believe anyone or anything else is causing these problems?\n\nPatient: Anyone else? No, no, no. Why would there be anyone else? It’s not some big, crazy conspiracy with the government or the neighborhood watch or the mailman. I’m not crazy, Doc. Like I keep saying—and I know I’m repeating myself, but I have to, because I’m only sitting in this office right now because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it! But I’m not. I know, for an absolute fact, it is just my wife’s relative. He came into this house specifically to get at me, and he’s doing it solo. \n\nThink about it logically. Why would he want to bring anybody else into it? If he brings someone else in, he’s got to share the spoils. And the prize here is my life. I mean, my tools don’t move themselves. We’ve established that. I don't have ghosts moving my calipers and my wrenches, I have a freeloader. And my fishing plans? They don’t just get spoiled by accident every single weekend by some mysterious force of nature or a string of bad luck. It’s him. Just him. He’s the one sneaking around with the superglue and the wire cutters. \n\nI’ve spent months—months, Doc—just observing this whole situation, keeping my mouth shut, taking mental notes of every little thing he does. And after all these months, I can see plain as day what his game is. He doesn't need anyone else's help because his plan is actually pretty simple. He’s been trying to take over my place and push me out. That’s it. That’s the whole grand scheme. He pushes me out, he gets my wife to think I've lost it, and then he gets to sit in my recliner, park in my garage, and use the tools I paid for with thirty years of sweat on a fabrication floor. \n\nSo no, there’s nobody else. Just one greedy, manipulative guy who wants what I built. And I'm not going to just roll over and let him have it, no matter how many doctors my wife makes me talk to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
