He figures if he drives me crazy enough, if he gets my wife to think I'm losing my marbles by complaining about things she refuses to see, then I'm the one who gets kicked out, or locked up, or whatever. And then what happens? He gets the run of the house. He gets my garage. He gets my life. \n\nSo, no. There is zero chance I'm mistaken. I wish I was, Doc, I really do. I know exactly what he's doing.\n\nDoctor: Besides your wife’s relative, do you believe anyone or anything else is causing these problems?\n\nPatient: Anyone else? No, no, no. Hearing or seeing things? Like... like voices in the walls or ghosts or something? Doc, again with this. We literally just talked about the sci-fi stuff. No. Absolutely not. 

My hearing is fine. Well, I mean, I've got a little bit of ringing in my left ear from the stamping presses back in the 90s—this was before the shop got really strict about the heavy-duty ear protection on the floor—but I hear what's actually there. I have to. In my line of work, if a bearing is going out on a band saw or a grinder, I can hear that high-pitched whine before anyone else on the floor even notices. That's real hearing. Real sound waves. Not voices in my head. 

And seeing things? My eyes are sharp. With my reading glasses, I can spot a bad weld or a hairline fracture in a piece of sheet metal from ten feet away. I don't see things that aren't there. If I see something, it's because it is physically occupying space in the real world. 

Now, look, if you're asking this because my wife says she doesn't see or hear the things I do... well, that's a whole different story, isn't it? When I hear a floorboard creak out in the hallway at two in the morning, it's not some imaginary phantom. It's him. It's that freeloader sneaking out to the garage to mess with my tackle box. My wife might tell you she didn't hear it, but she sleeps like a log, and frankly, she turns a blind eye to everything he does anyway! And when I see a shadow move past the window, or I see my socket set moved around on my workbench, it's a real shadow and real tools being moved by a real person. 

I just... I don't know why we keep going down this road. It's like you're just going down a checklist of crazy person things. 'Do you hear voices?' 'Do you see aliens?' No! I see a guy who is systematically trying to push me out of my own house. You want to know what I actually see? I see the smirk on his face the second my wife turns her back. I see the way he leaves his dirty boots exactly where I'm going to trip over them. I hear the way he mutters under his breath when I walk into the kitchen to get a cup of coffee. That's what I see and hear. Real things, Doc. Everyday, undeniable facts. Not visions, not voices, just the reality of what I'm dealing with every single day.

If you walked into your office here, right, and your pens were moved from the left side of your desk to the right, and your chair was lowered three inches, and your files were out of alphabetical order, would you just say, 'Oh, well, I guess I'm just losing my mind'? No, you'd know someone had been in here. \n\nThat's exactly what I'm dealing with. I have been an assembler for thirty-two years. Thirty-two years of putting things together, exactly where they belong, piece by piece. My brain works like a blueprint. I don't misplace a 5/8 inch wrench. I don't accidentally leave the chuck key out of the drill press. But I go out there, and it's moved. And my wife's relative—he was over here for Thanksgiving, right? And ever since then, it's been this slow, methodical campaign. \n\nIt’s not just the garage, like I said. It’s the fishing gear. Every Saturday morning, I wake up at 4:30 AM. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
The strongest reason? Well, it’s not just one single thing, Doc, it’s the whole... it’s the whole undeniable pattern of it all. I mean, like I said before, I’m only sitting here in this chair, talking to you right now, because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My wife, her family, they all look at me, shake their heads, and treat me like I’ve got a screw loose. But I know. I know for a fact that he came into this house specifically to get at me. 

You want the strongest proof? It’s the sheer physical impossibility of it being anything or anyone else. Look, my tools don’t move themselves. I’ve been a fabricator for thirty years, alright? You put a 3/8-inch drive ratchet in the top drawer of your Kennedy toolbox, it stays in the top drawer. It doesn't magically walk over to the far end of the workbench and hide itself under a pile of oily rags. A heavy-duty C-clamp doesn't just fall off a pegboard and wedge itself behind the air compressor. 

And my fishing plans? I plan those trips out to the letter. I check the weather, I prep the outboard motor, I sort the tackle box on Thursday night so I'm ready for Saturday morning. They don’t just get spoiled by accident every single weekend. You don't just find your favorite lures with the hooks snipped off by coincidence. You don't find water in the gas tank of the boat by a stroke of bad luck. You just don't. 

So when you ask for the strongest reason, it’s because I've done the math. I’ve watched him. I’ve spent months—literally months of my life—just sitting back, biting my tongue, and putting the pieces together, watching how he operates when he thinks I'm out of the room. And after all these months, I can see plain as day what his endgame is. It's not a random string of bad luck, and it's not me losing my memory. He’s been systematically trying to take over my place and push me out. 

That’s it right there. That’s the reason. It's the only logical conclusion when you look at the evidence. He wants me gone so he can have my house, my garage, my wife's undivided attention, all of it. He wants to break me down so I leave, or so they lock me up in a place like this. And the fact that nobody else will just open their eyes and look at the facts... well, that's exactly why I'm stuck here trying to explain basic common sense to you.

That is not a coincidence. After all these months of this happening, day in and day out, I can see plain as day what's going on. He’s been trying to take over my place. He wants to push me out of my own home, just edge me right out of the picture entirely, and nobody wants to listen to me about it. They just sit there and tell me I'm crazy, but I'm the one living it.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that this is really happening, if at all?\n\nPatient: How certain am I? Doc, I am one hundred percent, absolutely, unequivocally certain. There is not a single shadow of a doubt in my mind. I mean, look at it this way. If you walked into your office here, right, and your pens were moved from the left side of your desk to the right, and your chair was lowered three inches, and your files were out of alphabetical order, would you just say, 'Oh, well, I guess I'm just losing my mind'? No, you'd know someone had been in here. \n\nThat's exactly what I'm dealing with. I have been an assembler for thirty-two years. Thirty-two years of putting things together, exactly where they belong, piece by piece. My brain works like a blueprint. I don't misplace a 5/8 inch wrench. I don't accidentally leave the chuck key out of the drill press. But I go out there, and it's moved. And my wife's relative—he was over here for Thanksgiving, right? And ever since then, it's been this slow, methodical campaign. \n\nIt’s not just the garage, like I said. It’s the fishing gear. Every Saturday morning, I wake up at 4:30 AM. I make my coffee—black, no sugar—and I go out to load the truck. And every single time, without fail, something is wrong. Last week, the spark plug wires on my outboard motor were swapped. Swapped! You don't just accidentally swap spark plug wires. Two weeks before that, someone had let exactly ten pounds of air out of my front left tire. Just enough to be a problem, not enough to be obvious right away. \n\nAnd my wife, she just sighs and rolls her eyes and tells me I'm being paranoid. But I see what he's doing. It's so clear to me. He wants to make me look incompetent. He wants her to think I'm losing it, so he can step in, be the man of the house, and push me right out the door. I’ve seen the way he looks around my property, sizing it up. I know it sounds crazy to you, and I know it sounds crazy to my family, but I am living it every single day. I am as certain of this as I am that the sun is going to come up tomorrow morning. There is no 'if at all' about it. It is happening.\n\nDoctor: Do any other people in your family or community believe this is happening too?\n\nPatient: Not a single one of them, Doc. And that... well, that right there is exactly why I’m sitting in this chair talking to you today. I’m only here because everybody—and I mean everybody, my wife, my kids, the neighbors—keeps acting like I’m imagining the whole thing. It’s like talking to a brick wall. I try to sit my wife down, right? We'll be at the kitchen table, and I’ll say, 'Look at this. Look at how the workbench was left.' And she just sighs, gets that pitying look on her face, and tells me to let it go. Like I'm a child making up ghost stories in the dark. \n\nBut I know what I know. I know my wife’s relative came into this house specifically to get at me. It's not a theory, it's a fact. But they all just gang up and say, 'Oh, you're working too hard, you're just stressed out from the shop.' Stressed? Does stress pick up a heavy-duty torque wrench and move it to the opposite side of the garage? Because my tools don’t move themselves! I’ve been working with my hands for thirty-two years, I know how gravity works. \n\nAnd I try to tell them about the weekends, too. I lay it all out for them, completely logically. I explain that my fishing plans don’t get spoiled by accident every single weekend. Once? Sure, maybe you get a tangled line. Twice? Bad luck. But every weekend for months? You'd think after the fifth or sixth time the truck gets messed with, someone in my family would say, 'Hey, maybe he's onto something.' But no. They just look at me like I'm the one losing my grip. \n\nIt’s isolating, Doc, I’ll tell you that much. You work your whole life, providing for your family, keeping your house in perfect order, and then this happens. After all these months of this going on, I can see plain as day he’s been trying to take over my place. He wants to push me out. And as for communicating with me? If he wanted to communicate with me, he’d just open his mouth, but he doesn't have the guts to face me man-to-man. So he just sneaks around in the dark like a coward. \n\nIt’s all very basic, Doc. Very grounded. No lasers, no mind control, no hidden messages in the newspaper. Just a guy trespassing in my garage and messing with my life. You see the difference, right? I mean, I feel like you're trying to put me in a box with people who see UFOs and talk to ghosts, and that's just insulting. I deal in reality. Cold, hard, everyday reality. So no, no special powers. Just a very real problem living under my roof.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences of hearing or seeing things that other people around you could not?\n\nPatient: Hearing or seeing things? Like... like voices in the walls or ghosts or something? Doc, again with this. We literally just talked about the sci-fi stuff. No. Absolutely not. \n\nMy hearing is fine. Well, I mean, I've got a little bit of ringing in my left ear from the stamping presses back in the 90s—this was before the shop got really strict about the heavy-duty ear protection on the floor—but I hear what's actually there. I have to. In my line of work, if a bearing is going out on a band saw or a grinder, I can hear that high-pitched whine before anyone else on the floor even notices. That's real hearing. Real sound waves. Not voices in my head. \n\nAnd seeing things? My eyes are sharp. With my reading glasses, I can spot a bad weld or a hairline fracture in a piece of sheet metal from ten feet away. I don't see things that aren't there. If I see something, it's because it is physically occupying space in the real world. \n\nNow, look, if you're asking this because my wife says she doesn't see or hear the things I do... well, that's a whole different story, isn't it? When I hear a floorboard creak out in the hallway at two in the morning, it's not some imaginary phantom. It's him. It's that freeloader sneaking out to the garage to mess with my tackle box. My wife might tell you she didn't hear it, but she sleeps like a log, and frankly, she turns a blind eye to everything he does anyway! And when I see a shadow move past the window, or I see my socket set moved around on my workbench, it's a real shadow and real tools being moved by a real person. \n\nI just... I don't know why we keep going down this road. It's like you're just going down a checklist of crazy person things. 'Do you hear voices?' 'Do you see aliens?' No! I see a guy who is systematically trying to push me out of my own house. You want to know what I actually see? I see the smirk on his face the second my wife turns her back. I see the way he leaves his dirty boots exactly where I'm going to trip over them. I hear the way he mutters under his breath when I walk into the kitchen to get a cup of coffee. That's what I see and hear. Real things, Doc. Everyday, undeniable facts. Not visions, not voices, just the reality of what I'm dealing with every single day.\n\nDoctor: What is the strongest reason you think your wife’s relative is the one doing this?\n\nPatient: The strongest reason? Well, it’s not just one single thing, Doc, it’s the whole... it’s the whole undeniable pattern of it all. I mean, like I said before, I’m only sitting here in this chair, talking to you right now, because everybody keeps acting like I’m imagining it. My wife, her family, they all look at me, shake their heads, and treat me like I’ve got a screw loose. But I know. I know for a fact that he came into this house specifically to get at me. \n\nYou want the strongest proof? It’s the sheer physical impossibility of it being anything or anyone else. Look, my tools don’t move themselves. I’ve been a fabricator for thirty years, alright? You put a 3/8-inch drive ratchet in the top drawer of your Kennedy toolbox, it stays in the top drawer. It doesn't magically walk over to the far end of the workbench and hide itself under a pile of oily rags. A heavy-duty C-clamp doesn't just fall off a pegboard and wedge itself behind the air compressor. \n\nAnd my fishing plans? I plan those trips out to the letter. I check the weather, I prep the outboard motor, I sort the tackle box on Thursday night so I'm ready for Saturday morning. They don’t just get spoiled by accident every single weekend. You don't just find your favorite lures with the hooks snipped off by coincidence. You don't find water in the gas tank of the boat by a stroke of bad luck. You just don't. \n\nSo when you ask for the strongest reason, it’s because I've done the math. I’ve watched him. I’ve spent months—literally months of my life—just sitting back, biting my tongue, and putting the pieces together, watching how he operates when he thinks I'm out of the room. And after all these months, I can see plain as day what his endgame is. It's not a random string of bad luck, and it's not me losing my memory. He’s been systematically trying to take over my place and push me out. \n\nThat’s it right there. That’s the reason. It's the only logical conclusion when you look at the evidence. He wants me gone so he can have my house, my garage, my wife's undivided attention, all of it. He wants to break me down so I leave, or so they lock me up in a place like this. And the fact that nobody else will just open their eyes and look at the facts... well, that's exactly why I'm stuck here trying to explain basic common sense to you."
  }
